#3820. The Best Apple Watch for Most People Is Over $100 Off

The Apple Watch Series 10 was released in September 2024, and it's one of the best ways to get into the Apple Watch in 2026 without breaking the bank. Right now, the titanium 46 mm case model with cellular capabilities is $112 off, bringing the price down to $637 (originally $749). That's the lowest price after Apple raised prices this summer due to the RAM shortage, according to price-tracking tools. The same model in gold titanium is also over $100 off, reaching $679 (originally $799), saving you the same amount compared to the latest Series 12 going for $799.

The Apple Watch Series 10 was considered the best Apple Watch for most people last fall and is a great replacement if you're still wearing the more budget SE. There are also some key upgrades from the Apple Watch 9, although they're less noticeable than previous Apple Watch upgrades. If you're curious to know how it compares to other flagship smartwatches from its time, you can check out our head-to-head comparison.

Some of the first things you'll notice on the Series 10 are a thin case and a big OLED screen (either 1.6 inches or 1.8 inches, depending on which model you get). The Series 10 has audio playback, meaning you can listen to podcasts, take calls, or listen to Spotify on its speakers. The sleep features allow notifications for signs of sleep apnea. What you're giving up by saving on this version over the latest Series 12 is a faster S11 chip, a newer Health Sensing System and the Readiness app (tracks daily insights, vitals, and other metrics), a longer battery life (from 18 to 24 hours), 5G RedCap support (it helps save on battery life and has better network coverage compared to 5G), and audio intelligence features like Live Rewind, Siri Recap, and Sound Recognition.

If those extra features don't matter to you, the Series 10 is a great Apple Watch to get in 2026 at a discount, when many are still suffering from their price hikes.

#3814. "This is a serious incident" - Kingdom Come: Deliverance developer Warhorse suffers cyberattack

Studio working to determine "full extent".

Kingdom Come: Deliverance developer Warhorse Studios has confirmed it's suffered a "serious" cyberattack, saying it is currently working to determine the "full extent" of its impact.

"To our players and friends," a statement by the studio read. "We're sorry to share that Warhorse Studios has been hit by a cyberattack. We're working with cybersecurity specialists to secure our systems and understand the full extent of what happened. Thank you for standing with us." The message was released at 11am GMT and there hasn't been an update from the studio since. We've separately asked the studio if there have been any developments.

Warhorse CEO Martin Frývaldský discussed the incident in a Czech report (translated by DeepL), saying, "This is a serious incident for Warhorse Studios and for everyone affected. Our immediate priority is to secure our systems, determine the full extent of the breach, and take all necessary steps to protect our employees, partners, and our community. We are devoting all necessary resources and capabilities to resolving this situation."

Warhorse is currently working on two games, including a prestigious open-world Middle-earth role-playing game, based on Tolkien's legendary The Lord of the Rings fiction. It's also developing "a new Kingdom Come adventure", which we assume to be Kingdom Come: Deliverance 3. In addition, it's collaborating on a Kingdom Come: Deliverance film.

#3813. With War, Dominic West Solidifies Himself as Pop Culture’s Greatest Adulterer

War, the new HBO drama about a high-profile divorce between a movie star and a billionaire tech founder, opens with Dominic West cheating on Sienna Miller. Or, more accurately, Dominic West’s character of Morgan Henderson cheats on Sienna Miller’s character of Carla Duval. It’s a scene that feels scandalous but also, well, familiar.

It seems like West does his spouse dirty in every show he stars in. Behold the curriculum vitae: Jimmy McNulty in The Wire, a serial womanizer whose wife, Elena, leaves him due to his infidelity. In later seasons, he has an affair with the assistant state’s attorney. Noah Solloway in The Affair, which, as the title implies, is a show about a guy who cheats on his wife. Then there’s Prince Charles in The Crown. You know the story. (Then there’s the case of life possibly imitating art, when in 2020 West was spotted extracurricularly scootering around Rome with his then costar Lily James. Amid the ensuing tabloid storm, the actor walked outside his Cotswolds home with his wife Catherine FitzGerald. They left a note that read: “Our marriage is strong, and we’re very much still together. Thank you.” The moment was instantly memed.)

With all respect to West’s previous cheating-on-television canon, War might be his finest adulterous work. War is glossy, catty, and glamorous, swinging between multimillion-dollar townhomes in West London and high-end lawyer’s offices where they have retainers; the characters are so rich that they wear million-dollar watches and so bitchy they’ll stab each other in the back to get what they want. “Every man has his price,” says Morgan while drinking green juice at a five-star hotel. “And mine is prohibitively high.”

West plays his cheating roles deftly: He’s just smarmy enough that you believe his transgression, but not smarmy enough that you loathe him for it. In War, for example, Morgan Henderson does indeed cheat on his wife with a 20-something aspiring actress. But as it turns out, Morgan’s actually a pretty great stepdad, whereas Carla’s parenting skills have more…mixed reviews. (During a research and discovery session, Carla’s lawyers find out from a Hollywood director that “she wanted to get away from her family” and was “not exactly the maternal type.”) In The Crown, Prince Charles did humiliate Princess Diana. But Prince Philip pressured him into that marriage and Charles really, really loves Camilla Parker Bowles. (West himself admitted to The Sunday Times that his own tabloid drama shaped his role as Prince Charles. “I’d had a very acute understanding of what it’s like to feel the horror of your name or your photograph coming up in the newspapers,” he says.) As for Noah? The Affair is told with a dual his-and-hers narrative. Each episode shows the perspective of West’s Noah as well as his paramour, Ruth Wilson’s Allison. As a result, the viewer is unable to truly villainize either. As the saying goes, it takes two to tango.

Then there’s his looks: He’s rakish yet age-appropriate, straddling the delicate aesthetic line where you both understand why a woman might be attracted to him, but also that this is a man having a midlife crisis. “I have been made redundant—by my wife,” West’s Morgan grumbles to his business colleague.

Miller—who is no stranger to extramarital tabloid scandals herself—has also mastered her getting-cheated-on performances. In Anatomy of a Scandal, she played Sophie, the wife of a British cabinet member who is accused of having an affair with (and eventually sexually assaulting) his young aide. She tries to piece together the truth wearing a number of sumptuous Max Mara coats. You feel for Sophie—but you don’t pity her: The show delves into her psyche as she debates leaving her husband and the lifestyle he can provide. The final episode culminates in her starting a new life with her children in the country.

In the first episode, West’s Morgan serves divorce papers to Miller’s Carla at her own movie premiere.

Arguably part of the reason War is so delicious is that the two actors, at the top of their adulterous craft, are finally squaring off against each other. You root for both and therefore root for neither. You do root, however, for more gossipy drama to blow up in their faces. (Like when Morgan figures out Carla is filing for divorce because The Daily Mirror runs a paparazzi picture of her with lawyers in Paris—and rushes to beat her to filing.) But it’s also because, well, War deliberately plucks plot lines that pop in our pop-cultured riddled brains. On a macro level, that means the casting of West and Miller. On a micro level, it’s specific moments, like when Miller’s Carla gets served divorce papers at her own premiere—reminiscent of when Olivia Wilde got served custody papers while speaking onstage at CinemaCon.

Maybe that’s why War works so well. The tabloid business is based on our infinite fascination with imploding celebrity relationships: Who among us hasn’t clicked on something about Kim and Kanye, Jen and Brad, J.Lo and Ben, or Gwyneth and her “conscious uncoupling”? War allows that to scratch that lowbrow itch in our brains in a highbrow, HBO way—by casting two of the very celebrities we associate with such a scandal.

It also gives us more sordid details that we could have ever gossiped about. War brings us inside the lawyers boardrooms and peels back the pages of prenups of the rich and famous. A key plot point of War is that Carla wants their prenup thrown out, as Morgan got around $700 million richer during their marriage. Meanwhile, Morgan’s law firm, Cathcarts, is desperate to make Carla look like a frivolous, Marie Antoinette–level spender—cataloging that she spent 300 pounds on hand-cut Norwegian ice. Carla’s law firm, on the other hand, is desperate to cover up that the aforementioned 20-something Henderson cheated on their client with was a honey pot, planted by his wife who wants ammo for her divorce filing.

It’s the perfect low-stakes mess. Both characters are wealthy enough that even if their net worth’s got halved, they’d still live a lavish life; both characters are morally complicated enough that you don’t feel either of them are wronged. You just get to sit and watch it play out by the perfect couple. As West’s Morgan says about his own divorce: “This is horrible. And I’m very sorry about it!”

#3812. Kim Kardashian Recalls Last Conversation With Dad Robert Before His Death

Kim Kardashian is recalling the last conversation she had with her dad, Robert Kardashian, before his death in her new biography, Citizen Kim.

“The last thing my dad ever said to me was, ‘I know you’re going to be good — I can see it. It’s as if I can see what your whole life is going to be like,’” Kim, 46, shared in the book, written by journalist Jonathan Van Meter.

The Kardashians star continued, “My dad didn’t go into detail, and he was kind of in and out of it: ‘I can see it.’ He put his hand on my leg. I’ll never forget it. And I was laying next to him on this couch — this L-shaped couch — and we were kind of squished in this way, watching a movie. And he said, ‘You’ll be fine. But take care of the other ones. And make sure your brother’s good.’”

Kim also recalled her heartbreaking final visit with Robert before his death.

“He couldn’t communicate, but he was in his little bed, and I knew his vitals were almost gone, and the last thing I said to him was, ‘Dad, thank you so much for everything that you’ve taught me, from how to be a good person, to how you did my homework with me, everything I’m going to do with my kids one day. And I’m going to take the lesson of you being in my life and carry that forward,’” she said.

Kim continued, “Then the pastor came into the room and said, ‘Everyone should say goodbye because it’s only a matter of time, and he’s holding on because you girls are here. Maybe say your goodbyes and then leave the house, and he’ll pass away.’ And that’s exactly what we did, and that’s exactly what happened. I left his house and drove straight to Brandy [Norwood’s] house — so, thirty minutes. And I remember I was on Brandy’s bed and I got the phone call and I knew.” (Kardashian worked as a personal stylist for Norwood, 47, in 2003.)

Robert died in September 2003 at age 59 after a battle with esophageal cancer.

In addition to Kim, Robert shared daughters Kourtney Kardashian and Khloé Kardashian and son Rob Kardashian with ex-wife Kris Jenner.

Kim and her siblings have been candid about their father’s death over the years, including the impact his loss had on their lives.

“At the funeral, when I saw my father in his casket, I completely fell apart,” Khloé, 42, recalled in her 2015 memoir, Strong Looks Better Naked. ”I don’t remember the details, but apparently I was an emotional wreck, and I’m told that I was so distraught I actually passed out. At one point I fell to the floor kicking and screaming, and I had to be sedated. It was really intense. I refused to believe my father was gone.”

Kim has also credited Robert, an attorney who worked on the infamous O.J. Simpson murder trial, for inspiring her to study law.

“He once said to me, ‘I think you’d be great at it. But I also think that it’s super stressful and exhausting,” the reality star, who is currently taking a break from her law program after failing the bar exam in July 2025, told Vogue in 2019.

Kim reflected on growing up around her dad’s work during the Television Critics Association’s 2020 winter press tour.

“I think that by the time I was a teenager and he was working on the O.J. case, I was sneaking in his office, looking at all of the evidence and things I shouldn’t have been looking at,” she said. “Maybe it was in my soul for years that that’s what I would have wanted to do. And I’ve even seen interviews pop up now from six or seven years ago. People ask what I wanted to do if I wasn’t filming my show. I always said I wanted to be a crime scene investigator or an attorney.”

#3811. Elden Ring Movie Might Be Extremely Long, And It Could Have Twitch Integration Eventually

New details have come to light about A24's Elden Ring movie, including how Amazon could be involved and its beefy runtime.

According to Deadline, Amazon MGM is buying up the Elden Ring movie's Pay-1 streaming rights for numerous international markets in a deal potentially worth "tens of millions of dollars." The site reported that there could be "interesting activations" on Amazon's Twitch platform as part of the deal. A Pay-1 rights deal means the company gets exclusive rights to broadcast a movie after it ends its theatrical run and comes to home video.

Netflix, for example, paid Sony billions of dollars for a Pay-1 deal that includes the live-action Zelda movie. That was a global deal for many movies, though, and the report said Amazon MGM is booking an international deal specifically for the Elden Ring movie. It remains to be seen where the Elden Ring movie could stream after it wraps up in theaters.

The Elden Ring movie finished filming earlier this year and Industrial Light & Magic is now working on post-production efforts ahead of the movie's March 2028 release in theaters.

The film is based on From Software's game and was written and directed by Alex Garland, but the story details are being kept secret. Kit Connor is playing the lead, and the movie will apparently feature "battles between his character and impressive monsters and dragons."

The script is so secretive that A24 is reportedly booking flights for its staffers to potential international rights-buyers instead of sending the script digitally to ensure an A24 staffer is in the room as the script is being read. Additionally, the report said the script is at least 180 pages, meaning the film could stretch to a mighty three hours.

It was also reported that Elden Ring could become a movie franchise if the film does well, but no sequel plans are officially set yet.

In addition to Connor, the Elden Ring movie will star Cailee Spaeny, Ben Whishaw, Tom Burke, Jonathan Pryce, and Nick Offerman.

Finally, the Deadline report backed up the earlier rumor that the Elden Ring movie cost as much as $200 million to produce, making it A24's most expensive film ever.

#3809. What's New on Netflix in November 2026

Holiday content returns to Netflix in November, with the monthly slate weighted toward dozens of Christmas and seasonally themed films and shows as well as season nine of The Great British Baking Show: Holidays (Nov. 23). Netflix will also be hosting a Thanksgiving Eve NFL game with the Packers vs. Rams matchup on Nov. 25.

Other titles of note include The Altruists (Nov. 19), a limited drama series about the rise and fall—and crimes—of FTX founder Sam Bankman-Fried and Caroline Ellison (played by Julia Garner of Ozark and Inventing Anna fame). Jessica Chastain stars in the Netflix original film Heartland (Nov. 13), a thriller about a former country music star whose niece disappears.

On the documentary side, there's Ron Howard's Avedon (Nov. 11), a film about the photographer's life and influence, plus comedy specials from Mike Tyson (Return of the Mike, Nov. 3), Janelle James (How Romantic, Nov. 10), and Mike Ward (Modest, Nov. 17).

#3808. Pregnant Savannah Chrisley Details Fiancé Jason’s Elaborate Proposal

Savannah Chrisley shared the story behind how her fiancé Jason—with whom she’s also expecting her first baby—popped the question while on vacation in the Cayman Islands in December.

Savannah Chrisley is ringing in multiple milestones.

Not only is she expecting her first baby with her partner named Jason—whose identity she is otherwise keeping under wraps—but it turns out the pair are also engaged, with Jason having popped the question in December after five months of dating.

“Back in December, Jason and I were in the Cayman Islands and little did I know he had a ring in his backpack,” she said during the Oct. 27 episode of her Unlocked podcast. “Jason was getting a charger out of his backpack. I think I called him in the other room. He came in there, and then he left a box on the counter. I was like, ‘Oh my God! Is that what I think it is?’ But I didn't know to be 100 percent.”

Instead of jumping to conclusions, the 29-year-old tried to put the ring-shaped box out of her mind as she and Jason traveled to the island of Little Cayman.

“That morning, it was just the most magical morning,” she recalled. “We got up. It was super early. We go to the airport, and we're just so lovey with each other. It's the safest feeling I've ever felt in my entire life.”

She added, “We go to Little Cayman, we get on these kayaks. We had a little cooler and a speaker, and we went out in the middle of the ocean, and it was, like, literally the water was up to our waist. It was insane, and we sat there literally like for hours listening to music, and we talked and we explored all these little bitty islands. It was the coolest thing ever.”

As it turns out, Jason decided the day and setting was too picture-perfect not to propose there, frantically texting the couple’s friend Ashley for help getting the ring from his hotel room to the little island. And she delivered, not only successfully bringing the ring but also setting up a romantic moment for the pair.

“We come up to shore, and he's like, ‘Hey, babe, come over here,’ and I see flowers and champagne, and I just think he's being sweet,” Savannah explained. “Because keep in mind, I had been in the ocean all day. I was in a swimsuit. I had hair, makeup not done, none of that. So, I had no idea. I was just like, ‘Oh my god, he's being sweet.’”

After pouring Savannah champagne, Jason retrieved the ring from where Ashley had hidden the box beneath a palm tree.

“When he gave me the ring, he was like, ‘Before I open this, I just want you to know that this is just an expression of love to you,’” the Growing Up Chrisley alum recalled of the proposal. “‘This can be whatever you want it to be. This can be an engagement ring, a promise ring, a cocktail ring, for all I care. I just want you to know how much I love you.’ And there was no pressure ever put on me to like, you have to accept this.”

Nevertheless, “obviously I wanted to,” she added. “Like, yes it happened really quick, but it is what it is, right? Like timing means nothing. I feel like you could find out more about a person in three months than you did in a whole year of being with them.”

And despite having always said she wanted hair, makeup and the whole nine yards to be done when someone officially popped the question, Savannah thinks her and Jason’s special moment was absolutely perfect.

“That proposal was just right. It was everything,” she emphasized. “I would not trade that moment for anything in the entire world because it was us. It was us in our happy place, and it was such a spur of the moment thing for him because he's like, ‘Wait, I need to do it here. Like, this is us.’ And for that, I absolutely love him.”

#3806. Kris Jenner Channels Big Scorpio Energy in Elizabeth Taylor's Cape

Kris Jenner was only 14 years old when Grace Kelly organized her legendary Scorpio Ball in Monte Carlo, but the themed party made enough of an impact that the famous matriarch snagged a piece of it decades later—but waited until this weekend to show it off.

Before we get to that, let's turn the clock back to November, 1969, when Princess Grace threw a grand party to celebrate her 40th birthday. While the guest list was ostensibly restricted to guests who, like the Rear Window star, were born under the zodiac sign of Scorpio, non-Scorpios were allowed as plus-ones. For example, Richard Burton, born under that same zodiac sign on November 10, was accompanied by then-wife, Elizabeth Taylor, who—despite being a Pisces—commissioned the Roman fashion house Tiziani to create a stunning black velvet hooded cape for the occasion, featuring scorpions embroidered with rhinestones on the chest and hood.

Elizabeth Taylor at Grace Kelly’s Scorpio Ball, 1969

Fifty-seven years later, the cape has resurfaced in front of the paparazzi at a major party. Saturday night, it was spotted on the shoulders of Kris Jenner, who, accompanied by daughter Kim Kardashian and Kardashian's All's Fair cast-mates Niecy Nash, Naomi Watts, and Sarah Paulson, attended the Rome Film Festival premiere of the new season of the series.

In fact, it was in this series about female divorce lawyers that Elizabeth Taylor’s old cape was last viewed. Its creator, Ryan Murphy, decided to include an episode inspired by the famous 1969 Monegasque ball, only this time, the character played by Kardashian was the host. Jenner's daughter wore the iconic cape in the episode, which she has now confirmed was the original. and was lent to her by her mother.

Sarah Paulson, Kris Jenner, Kim Kardashian, Niecy Nash and Naomi Watts at the All's Fair screening at the 21st Rome Film Festival on October 24, 2026.

Although it is unknown how long she has owned it, Kris Jenner—a Scorpio, just like Grace Kelly—may have purchased it at the historic auction of Elizabeth Taylor’s collection that Christie’s organized in 2011. According to the auction house’s records, the garment sold for $60,000 at the time—a figure significantly higher than the starting price (experts had estimated it at $2,000–$3,000) a price that was perhaps influenced not only by the legend of Elizabeth Taylor but also by the workshop legend surrounding it.

According to Christie’s, in the late 1960s, the Tiziani fashion house counted among its ranks a young and still relatively unknown Karl Lagerfeld, and while the attribution of this particular garment to the designer has never been confirmed, the mere possibility might have been enough to increase its value and spark the interest of the Kardashian matriarch, who is admittedly “obsessed” with the House of Chanel.

Jenner's fascination with this type of historical piece is shared by at least one of her children. At the 2022 Met Gala, Kim Kardashian—who Saturday night opted for a vintage leopard-print Yves Saint Laurent design—appeared in the dress Marilyn Monroe wore to sing “Happy Birthday” to president John F. Kennedy in 1962, which she borrowed for the occasion from the Ripley’s Believe It or Not! museum.

Likewise, in her debut as a Balenciaga model, Kardashian walked the runway adorned with diamond jewelry owned Lorraine Schwartz that had belonged to Elizabeth Taylor. And just last weekend, during the Academy Museum of Motion Pictures Gala, Kardashian once again paid tribute to Taylor by wearing a 1951 Balenciaga archive design that evoked Taylor's silhouette and unmistakable style.

Originally published by Vanity Fair Spain

#3804. The Lesson Natalie Portman Learned After Her First Star Wars Movie Was Panned: ‘That Got Murdered’

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Discover the latest entertainment updates and trailers.

Read reviews, interviews and deep dives on new releases.

Get the latest movie, TV and streaming news delivered to your inbox.

I'm Really Bummed Jenna Ortega's New Movie Falls In Line With A 'Rotten' Trend She's On With Critics

Disney Is Betting Big With Its New Star Wars: Starfighter Movie (And Yes We Mean The Price Tag)

Pedro Pascal Doesn’t Hold Back About Taking ‘A Lot Of S--t’ In Hollywood Before Becoming A Star

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

There are few movie franchises with the cultural impact of the Star Wars movies. Generations grew up with the galaxy far, far away, both on the big screen and streaming with a Disney+ subscription. Actress Natalie Portman famously played Padmé in the prequel trilogy, and recalled a big lesson she learned when The Phantom Menace got panned by critics.

The pressure was on for Star Wars' prequel trilogy when it hit theaters, given it was the franchise's first time back on the big screen since George Lucas' originals. In an interview with Harper's Bazaar, the Oscar-winning actress spoke about why she was "lucky" for the The Phantom Menace's poor reviews. In her words:

I had lucky early experiences of failure. I know I’ve been also very lucky to have a lot of successes that have balanced it out. But I mean, for example, my first film, The Professional, got horrible reviews and had no box office. And then I made Star Wars. I was 16, and that got murdered. It was a huge success financially, but all of it was ‘They ruined the franchise. None of them can act. They all suck.’

Portman grew up in front of our eyes, and was introduced to professional challenges as a young person. As she pointed out, both The Professional and Star Wars: The Phantom Menace got ripped apart upon their release, even though the latter made a ton of money at the box office. Indeed, it took years for fans to start appreciating Episode I. One can only imagine how challenging this must have been at the time, especially when the cast's acting talents were put into question.

Later in the same interview, the Black Swan actress shared why this experience was actually helpful to her as she continued to work. As she put it:

I think it was an amazing early lesson in how to read really bad things about yourself and then be able to be like, ‘I’m going to make another one. I’m going to show them.’ I don’t remember talking to anyone about it. I didn’t want to show anyone that I was hurt or anything.

Honestly, I see her point. Rather than letting poor reviews defeat her or diminish her career, Natalie Portman just kept working. She refused to show that the critics upsetting her, and instead kept racking up major movie roles. Indeed, the Jackie actress has remained an A-lister in the years since starring as Padmé Amidala.

Portman would reprise her role in both of The Phantom Menace's sequels, titled Attack of the Clones and Revenge of the Sith. She's been away from the franchise since 2005, but there are plenty of fans who have been hoping to see her reprise her role on the silver or small screens. Only time will tell if that ever happens.

Star Wars will return to the big screen when Starfighter hits theaters on May 28th, 2027. Since it's not part of the 2026 movie release list, fans will have to re-watch the previous movies on Disney+.

Corey was born and raised in New Jersey. Graduated with degrees theater and literature from Ramapo College of New Jersey. After working in administrative theater for a year in New York, he started as the Weekend Editor at CinemaBlend. He's since been able to work himself up to reviews, phoners, and press junkets-- and is now able to appear on camera with some of his favorite actors... just not as he would have predicted as a kid. He's particularly proud of covering horror franchises like Scream and Halloween, as well as movie musicals like West Side Story. Favorite interviews include Steven Spielberg, Spike Lee, Jamie Lee Curtis, and more.

#3803. JetBlue (JBLU) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

JetBlue Airways Corporation (JBLU +18.99%) reported its third-quarter financial results, citing its path toward sustained operating profitability in 2027 and a target of at least $1 in earnings per share by 2028. Management indicated that revenue performance was supported by higher yields and the expansion of the Fort Lauderdale hub, which is becoming a major connecting operation for the airline. The company expects margin expansion to continue into the fourth quarter, driven by the implementation of commercial initiatives under the JetForward plan and a projected decrease in non-fuel unit costs. Financial flexibility is maintained through a liquidity bridge that includes a pool of unencumbered aircraft and engines valued at approximately $2.8 billion.

Operator: Good morning, everyone. My name is Jillian. I would like to welcome everyone to the JetBlue Airways third quarter 2026 earnings conference call. As a reminder, today's call is being recorded. At this time, all participants are in a listen only mode. I would now like to turn the call over to JetBlue's director of investor relations, Koosh Rohit Patel. Please go ahead, sir.

Koosh Rohit Patel: Thanks, Jillian. Good morning, everyone, thank you for joining us for our third quarter 2026 earnings call. Yesterday, we issued our earnings release and a presentation that we will reference during this call. All of those documents are available on our website at investor.jetblue.com, on the SEC's website at www.sec.gov. In New York, to discuss our results are Joanna L. Geraghty, our Chief Executive Officer; Martin J. George, our president and Ursula L. Hurley, our chief financial officer. During today's call, we will make forward looking statements about our outlook, strategy, and future performance. These statements are based on our current expectations and are subject to risks and uncertainties that could cause actual results to differ materially.

Please refer to our earnings release and SEC filings for information about risk factors that could cause those differences. These statements speak only as of today, and we undertake no obligation to update them. We may also discuss certain non GAAP financial measures. Reconciliations to the most directly comparable GAAP measures are included in our earnings materials and are available on our Investor Relations website. And now I would like to turn the call over to Joanna L. Geraghty, JetBlue's CEO.

Joanna L. Geraghty: Thank you, Kush. Good morning, everyone, and thank you for joining us today. I want to start by reinforcing the bigger picture. The progress that we are making. Despite significant fuel headwinds and a challenging summer operation, our revenue performance is among the strongest in the industry. With unit revenue up 18.4% in the third quarter and further acceleration expected in the fourth. Customer demand remains resilient. We are seeing JetForward benefits compounding with some of our largest opportunities still ahead. Importantly, we are already expanding margins year-over-year despite elevated fuel prices. We remain focused on operating profitability in 2027 and we see multiple paths to at least $1 in earnings per share and positive free cash flow in 2028.

Importantly, our liquidity and financing flexibility provide an important bridge as these benefits build. The fuel challenges the industry faces are real. But so is our progress. JetBlue has a strong brand, a high value network customers who are loyal to our differentiated offering, great crew members and a clear strategy to achieve sustained profitability. We are confident in our plan and focused on delivering the results that this airline is capable of achieving. Turning to the third quarter. I am very encouraged by the strength we saw in the underlying business this quarter. Revenue materially exceeded our initial expectations and strengthened as the quarter progressed, with momentum continuing into the fourth quarter.

For me, 2 highlights stand out from the quarter. First, demand remained resilient even as industry fares moved higher in response to elevated jet fuel costs. This revenue strength helped us recapture higher fuel, faster than initially expected, and we achieved 80% recapture in the third quarter versus our initial expectation of 70% to 80%. Second, Fort Lauderdale continues to demonstrate the strength of our network strategy. Delivering strong unit revenue performance even as we significantly expanded our operation. At the same time, the quarter was impacted by an unusually challenging summer operating environment. Severe weather and air traffic control constraints across our core Northeast geographies reduced our completed capacity and increased our disruption in crew costs.

Through it all, our crew members worked tirelessly to care for our customers and recover our operations. I want to thank them for their tremendous dedication throughout such a demanding summer. Even with elevated fuel prices, and the air traffic control pressures we faced this summer, we still expect to deliver year-over-year adjusted operating margin expansion over the second half of this year. We saw modest margin expansion in the third quarter and expect 1 to 2 points of further growth in the fourth quarter. Even with fuel up 85%. We know there is still work ahead. Particularly given elevated fuel prices and macroeconomic uncertainty. But our third quarter performance provides further evidence that JetForward is delivering results.

We have now delivered 4 consecutive quarters of improving year-over-year RASM growth. And are among the industry leaders for second half revenue expansion. We remain on track to achieve our 2026 JetForward target with several important initiatives continuing to build into 2027 and beyond. And we are already seeing that execution translate into results in Fort Lauderdale. We expect our seat share to reach approximately 37% in the fourth quarter. Up 14 points year-over-year, Reflecting the scale that we are building as our operation expands. We are also deepening our engagement with local customers and co brand acquisitions in Broward County increased 55% year over year in the third quarter.

These actions are driving strong RASM growth in Fort Lauderdale and illustrate the early success of our strategic capacity growth. Blue first is yet another important driver of our growth. And is our largest Jet Forward initiative. Seats are now on sale, and we look forward to welcoming our first customers in early December. We are excited to offer customers a domestic first class experience at an accessible price point. At runway, we expect BlueFirst to deliver nearly 5 points of RASM improvement with its revenue and margin contributions building through 2028 and beyond. Turning to our financial position.

We ended the quarter with $2.2 billion in liquidity, and approximately $7 billion in unencumbered assets, including $2.8 billion in aircraft and engines. Together, these resources provide substantial financial flexibility and an important bridge as we execute JetForward and move toward a stronger earnings and cash flow profile. The progress that we are seeing across our business is real. Our revenue initiatives are delivering. We are beginning to expand margins year-over-year, And the benefits of Jet Forward are compounding as existing initiatives mature and new ones come online.

With the financial resources to support our plan, our focus is on building on that momentum to achieve sustained operating profitability in 2027 and achieve positive free cash flow and at least $1 of earnings per share in 2028. Let me be clear, that is where our attention is, and that is how we intend to create long term value for our shareholders. With that, I will turn it over to Martin.

Martin J. George: Thank you, Joanna. I also want to thank our crew members for their unwavering commitment to our customers and to JetBlue throughout some. Efforts helped us navigate a challenging operating environment while continuing to deliver strong commercial results. Revenue was a clear highlight in the third quarter. We are pleased that the strikes we saw through September has carried into the fourth quarter. During Q3, RASM increased 18.4% year-over-year, Roughly 4 points better than the midpoint of our initial guidance. That puts JetBlue among the industry leaders in year over year total and unit revenue growth this quarter. Reinforcing the momentum that we have seen with our commercial initiatives.

Higher yields supported in part by our investments in revenue management technology, drove a majority of this outperformance. Customer volumes remained relatively resilient and we saw no meaningful signs elasticity despite higher fares. As we responded to elevated fuel costs. Both premium and main delivered double digit RASM growth. With the strength in Maine particularly encouraging. Demand strength remained broad based across both peak and off peak travel periods, with healthy booking trends continuing into the fall. Turning to Fort Lauderdale, We see continued acceleration in RASM growth throughout the year, even as we significantly expanded our operation. In the third quarter, RASM increased nearly 15% year over year on more than 65% capacity growth.

This performance demonstrates strong customer response as we continue to rapidly build scale in the market. We are expanding travel options for Broward County residents and creating more connecting opportunities for customers throughout our network by adding destinations and frequencies and optimizing our bank schedule. With 15 new destinations, increased frequencies to others, we expect peak daily departures to exceed 150 this winter. Up 39% year-over-year, We recently launched service to 2 new destinations in Colombia, bringing our international network from Fort Lauderdale to 18 destinations. Robust growth requires close collaboration. I want to personally thank Matt Gale, and the team at Broward County Aviation Department for their ongoing partnership as we continue to expand our operations.

While we plan to continue to grow, we believe the third quarter likely represents our peak year over year growth rate in Fort Lauderdale. This should support further RASM expansion as our growth moderates and new capacity matures. Transitioning to BlueFirst, our new domestic first class experience is now on sale across more than 30 routes. Service begins December 8 between Boston and Fort Lauderdale. And will expand to additional routes in a phased rollout. We prioritize markets with strong premium demand. Where BlueFirst can help us capture high yield bookings and improve our revenue mix. We are encouraged by the early response to BlueFirst.

Which suggests that we are capping independent demand for a domestic first-class product among existing customers. Particularly on leisure routes from New York and Boston. Approximately 35% of bookings so far are award redemptions. And Mosaic members account for nearly a quarter of the seats booked. While still early, this engagement reinforces our confidence in BlueFirst's ability to capture more premium travel demand. From our existing loyal customer base. We are also pleased with the progress that we are making in modernizing our revenue management technology. We have completed the transition to a new system that improves forecasting, and enables more sophisticated revenue optimization across our network. Including automated management of MIP and connecting itineraries for the first time.

Early results are ahead of our expectations, and we are adding capabilities to better anticipate shifts in demand. Respond more quickly to market conditions, support further unit revenue improvement over time. Our loyalty to deliver strong sequential growth. Driven by a premier. I see our premium and partners strategy. Revenue up 20% for the quarter, our highest level of growth since 2023. Acquisitions of the Premier Card, grew 8-fold year-over-year in the third quarter, as customers responded positively to the benefits refresh and the opening of our latest Blue House in Boston. Card spend remained robust across our co brand portfolio, growing double digits. And pulling loyalty cash remuneration solidly on track for mid teens growth for the year.

Our investments in premium products and partnerships are driving deeper brand loyalty and engagement, record levels of active TrueBlue members and elite status qualifications this quarter. Our newest Blue House launch has been met by rave reviews, outstanding NPS results since opening in August. And it is just another 1 of the many investments we are making in the premium customer experience. Our Blue Sky Loyalty Collaboration which improves global access with TrueBlue members, has now passed more than 1.1 billion TrueBlue points redeemed on United flights since the loyalty assets launched in February. We are also seeing encouraging progress at Paisly. Delivered its strongest financial result ever in Q3.

Further, rental car bookings through MileagePlus travel have outpaced United's previous provider on a per day basis since launch. We are building on that early traction with plans to introduce additional offerings throughout 2026 and the first half of 27. Paisley is on track to deliver nearly $100 million in operating income this year, with that contribution expected to nearly double by 2028. Driven by the maturation of our existing JetBlue business, and the continued ramp of planned offerings in United. Importantly, this target does not assume any additional partners which presents a potential source of further upside as we expand the platform.

Looking forward, we expect fourth quarter RASM to increase 18% to 22% year-over-year, This represents further acceleration from the third quarter. Despite year-over-year comparisons that are 2 to 3 points more challenging as we begin to lap the demand improvement that started late last year. This is expected to be our fifth consecutive quarter of escalating year-over-year RASM growth. Which provides further evidence that our JetForward initiatives are delivering results and strengthening our revenue performance. Based on what our competitors have guided so far, we expect to continue to be among industry leaders in the fourth quarter as well. For the full year, we now expect RASM to increase 13.5% to 14.5%.

Even with this revenue momentum, we remain disciplined with our capacity deployment. Fourth quarter growth is concentrated in Fort Lauderdale, with initial return and substantial opportunity to continue to support additional investment. Capacity in the rest of the network is flat to down. We expect fourth quarter capacity growth of 3% to 4%. And full year growth of 1.5% to 6.5% year-over-year, Our fourth quarter capacity guide reflects the significant impact of weather and operational disruptions that reduced completed flying last year. Excluding the impact of those prior year disruptions, scheduled capacity growth is nearly 2 points lower.

Taken together, continued revenue strength, disciplined capacity deployment, and the growing contributions from our JetForward initiatives give us confidence in our revenue trajectory as we close out the year and head into 2027. And with that, I will turn it over to Ursula.

Ursula L. Hurley: Thank you, Martin. We are encouraged by the progress we are making on margins. We delivered year-over-year adjusted operating margin improvement in the third quarter and expect further expansion in the fourth. As continued revenue strength is complemented by a meaningful step down in the growth of our CASM fuel. This progression is an important step towards sustained operating profitability in 2027. We are also taking concrete steps to further enhance liquidity and maintain the financial flexibility needed to execute JetForward. On the cost side, third quarter CASM-ex fuel increased 7% year-over-year, At the midpoint of our updated September guidance.

As previously communicated, our underlying unit cost performance remained consistent with our initial 2.5% to 4.5% guidance range, excluding approximately 4 points of pressure from summer operating disruption. Our third quarter fuel price was $3.91 per gallon. 57% higher year-over-year, Looking to the fourth quarter, we expect fuel prices of approximately $4.64 per gallon, with continued revenue strength supporting further progress in recapturing higher fuel costs. Looking to the fourth quarter, we expect CASM-ex fuel between negative 1% and positive 1% year-over-year, This represents a meaningful step down in unit cost growth and a return to the cost trajectory we guided at the beginning of the year.

For the full year, we now expect CASM-ex fuel growth of 3.5% to 4.5%. Turning to profitability. We now expect a full year adjusted operating margin of negative 4.5% to 6.5%. The revision primarily reflects higher fuel prices, which created 3.5 points of pressure relative to our July outlook. Stronger revenue performance has helped offset a significant portion of these headwinds. We also expect 1 to 2 points of year-over-year adjusted operating margin expansion in the fourth quarter. Despite fuel prices expected to be 85% higher than last year. This reflects the strength of our revenue initiatives and continued improvement in our underlying cost trajectory. Turning to liquidity. Our position remains strong.

And we continue to take a disciplined and proactive approach to managing our balance sheet. Our quarter end liquidity was $2.2 billion including our $600 million undrawn credit facility. We also provided notice to draw the incremental $250 million available under our aircraft backed financing accordion with funding expected in the fourth quarter. We have recently completed updated appraisals across our fleet, positioning us well for potential additional aircraft backed financing. Higher appraised values together with 8 A220 aircraft purchased with cash this year through the third quarter, bring the value of our fully unencumbered aircraft and engines to $2.8 billion. Up from roughly $2 billion previously.

This brings our total unencumbered asset base to approximately $7 billion Importantly, our portfolio of unencumbered aircraft and engines includes a similar mix of assets as those we successfully financed earlier this year. Together with our existing liquidity, our financing options give us confidence that we have the flexibility and runway to navigate a prolonged period of higher fuel prices while continuing to support our operations and advance JetForward. Turning to capital expenditures. Third quarter CapEx was $189 million below our prior outlook primarily due to the timing of 1 aircraft delivery and LGA slot payments shifting into the fourth quarter.

Our full year CapEx outlook remains unchanged at approximately $850 million We continue to expect annual CapEx below $1 billion through the end of the decade. Supporting our path towards positive free cash flow and balance sheet improvement. Looking ahead, we expect the benefits of JetForward to continue compounding as our existing commercial initiatives mature, BlueFirst begins to contribute and cost efficiencies build. That growing contribution supports our goal of achieving operating profitability in 2027 progressing towards positive free cash flow. Further out, we continue to target at least $1 of earnings per share in 2028.

While the mix of drivers may differ from what we outlined in July, our strong revenue performance reinforces our confidence that there are multiple paths to achieving that target. Including through stronger unit revenues that could help offset potentially higher fuel costs. Despite the challenging macro environment, we are starting to see financial progress and adjusted operating margin expansion. We still have plenty of work to do but the path is becoming clear. We expect positive free cash flow in 2028 and believe we have the liquidity and unencumbered asset base needed to get us there. We will remain focused on disciplined execution and capital management to drive sustainable earnings growth and create long term value for our shareholders.

With that, we are happy to take your questions. Over to you, Jillian.

Operator: Thank you. We will now begin the question and answer session. Please limit yourself to 1 question and 1 follow-up. If you would like to ask a question, please press *1 to raise your hand. #3802. GTA 6 File Size Revealed, And It’s Massive

Ahead of the game's release in November, GTA 6's file size has been revealed, and it is massive. PlayStation Game Size reports that the $80 standard edition will take up 210GB of storage capacity.

Size details for the $100 Ultimate Edition--which includes a ton of content not in the regular version--were not made available. We also don't know the Xbox Series X|S file size, but it's likely around the same.

As announced previously, preloading for GTA 6 begins on November 12, which is good news considering the game is massive in terms of its file size. People will no doubt have to delete other content to fit the game on the PS5 or Xbox Series X|S. This is double the file size of Rockstar's last game, Red Dead Redemption II.

Everyone expected GTA 6's file size to be gigantic, but now there is a number behind it, and it's one that makes it among the largest games ever in terms of file size. GTA 6's map is three times bigger than Red Dead Redemption 2's, and the game is filled with lots of detail in the vein of that game's horse testicles.

GTA 6 launches on November 19 for PS5 and Xbox Series X|S, and it's expected to be the biggest game of all time. One report said the game could hit 25 million preorders by launch, generating $2 billion in revenue.

GTA 6 is a strictly single-player game, but it's safe to assume that Rockstar will eventually release a new version of GTA Online. A PC edition, meanwhile, is expected in early 2027.

#3801. Matthew Perry's Ex Claims They Joined Mile High Club on Bruce Willis' Plane

Matthew Perry‘s ex-girlfriend reflected on their romance — and made a surprise sex confession — in Netflix’s three-part docuseries about his life.

“I was in love with him because he’s, at the core, good people,” René Ashton, who dated Perry in 1999, shared in The One About Matthew Perry, which premiered Tuesday, October 27.

Ashton, 56, recalled Perry struggling with his career amid his massive success on Friends, adding, “When you think of Matthew Perry you think, ‘Oh, he had it all.’ But Matthew was concerned about what’s next. [He was like], ‘What’s next? How am I gonna maintain this? How am I gonna supersede this?’  Matthew wanted very much to be a serious actor. He wanted to be known as a serious actor. That was really important to him.”

The actress specifically pointed to Perry’s work on the 2000 film The Whole Nine Yards.

“Before he went to shoot The Whole Nine Yards, Bruce asked him and I to go to Turks and Caicos with him and his then-girlfriend,” Ashton shared. “It was pretty amazing. There weren’t any paparazzi there so it was a nice, normal and fun trip.”

She continued, “And when Bruce asked him and I to go on his private plane, Matthew was so excited. He’s like, ‘Bruce wants us to go on his private plane. Bruce wants me to share a plane with him.'”

Ashton was asked by an offscreen producer what the plane was like, to which she coyly replied, “I joined a club.”

The One About Matthew Perry is a three-part special that explores Perry’s life in and behind the spotlight. In addition to examining his rise to fame with the role of Chandler Bing on Friends, the doc addressed the late actor’s private struggles with addiction, mental health and attempts to get sober before his shocking death at age 54 in October 2023.

At the time, Us confirmed that officers responded to a call regarding a person in cardiac arrest at a Los Angeles home, where they found the actor unconscious in his hot tub.

Two months after Perry’s death, a toxicology report from the Los Angeles County Medical Examiner’s Office determined the actor died from the acute effects of ketamine, a dissociative general anesthetic and NMDA receptor antagonist some have used to treat depression, anxiety and addiction.

The same report listed drowning, coronary artery disease and the effects of buprenorphine — a medication that is also used to treat addiction — as contributing factors to the actor’s death.

Perry’s death was ruled an accident, and an initial investigation was closed in January 2024 before it was later reopened. Five people have since faced federal charges in connection to Perry’s death — including his assistant Kenneth Iwamasa, who pleaded guilty to one count of conspiracy to distribute ketamine causing death.

“Kenny has known my brother for 30 years. He knows what he is batting. The level of betrayal we all felt … he should have known better,” Perry’s half-sister Mia Perry Bowick said in Tuesday’s doc. “He could have quit his job or told one of the family members. He could have done a lot of different things and made a lot of different choices.”

Jasveen Sangka, known as “The Ketamine Queen,” pleaded guilty to three counts of distribution of ketamine, one count of distribution of ketamine resulting in death or serious bodily injury and one count of using her home for drug distribution.

Erik Fleming also pleaded guilty to one count of distribution of ketamine resulting in death. Dr. Salvador Plasencia pleaded guilty to four counts of distribution of ketamine and Dr. Mark Chavez pleaded guilty to one count of conspiracy to distribute ketamine.

If you or someone you know is struggling with substance abuse, contact the Substance Abuse and Mental Health Services Administration (SAMHSA) National Helpline at 1-800-662-HELP (4357).

#3800. Why Jessica Biel Didn't Want Son Silas Having a Bigger Matchbox Role

Jessica Biel shared insight into how she and husband Justin Timberlake ensured their son Silas—who made a silent cameo in her Matchbox: The Movie—didn't expect special treatment as a nepo baby.

Jessica Biel isn't crying a river over her son's acting career.

Although the 7th Heaven alum and husband Justin Timberlake's eldest son Silas, 11, made a brief cameo in her new movie Matchbox, he can say bye, bye, bye to ever leaning on their family name for a leg up.

"There are kids who work really hard, and they go to acting class and they're in theater class and they're doing all the work," Biel recalled telling their son during her Oct. 22 appearance on the Josh Smith's Great Chat Show podcast. "You can't just take a part from that person just because you're my son. That's not cool."

"We're not doing that," she continued. "This is a special opportunity because it's really teeny tiny. If you had a big monologue, I wouldn't let him do it. You would have to earn that part because we're not starting that."

Silas made a small appearance at the end of the Apple TV movie, where Biel's character Aimee asked the silent tween to get out of a truck. But the actress isn't interested in letting Silas or their younger son Phineas, 6, be entitled nepo babies.

As she put it, "We're not starting like, 'Oh, you just get handed a bunch of opportunities because you're part of this family."

But Silas quickly learned that being a working actor isn't necessarily heavenly.

"The first day he was on set, he didn't work at all," she explained. "He was so upset about it. He was like, 'I just don't understand. I've been here all day.' He got really mad about it. I was like, 'This is what this is.'"

"They will call you in and they will sit for 12 hours," she told him. "It was the best lesson. And then I reminded him he was getting paid, and he was like, 'Oh, OK. I'm fine with this.'"

That said, Silas' time on set still hit all the right notes. After all, he even got to meet her costar John Cena.

"They're so funny," Biel told E! News earlier this month. "I feel like their brains implode whenever they come to set, especially a set like this, where there's a hero of theirs that they're meeting."

#3799. This Ultra-Wide Samsung Gaming Monitor Is $600 Off

An ultra-wide curved gaming monitor with ridiculous specs is every gamer's dream, but they (usually) don't come cheap. The 57-inch Samsung Odyssey Neo G9, one of the best ultra-wide gaming monitors on the market, is currently on sale for $1,499.99 (originally $2,499.99 at launch)—that's its second-best discount, according to price-checking tools.

The Samsung Odyssey Neo G9 Series is not a regular ultra-wide gaming monitor. It has impressive specs, but you'll need a top-notch gaming computer with specific outputs to take full advantage of its 240Hz refresh rate potential.

This monitor came out in 2023 with an "excellent" review from PCMag, mainly due to its impressive 7,680 by 2,160 4K native resolution, the ridiculous 57-inch screen size, its great color range and accuracy, the 240Hz refresh rate, and its design. The VA panel also means you get a bright 1,000-nit screen. To make full use of its powerful 240Hz refresh rate, you'll need to use a DisplayPort 2.1, or you'll be limited to 120Hz. You'll also need a powerful modern graphics card that is able to push the frames at a native resolution to max out the monitor's potential.

Although it is pricey, this monitor is a great value for the right gamer. You'll need plenty of space to fit a 57-inch screen, as well as a powerful computer to justify using the monitor—if you're not a competitive gamer with a powerful computer, it's probably not worth the price tag. Instead, consider this Asus gaming monitor going for $329.99 (originally $499) right now.

#3798. Queen Camilla Sparkles in an Heirloom Tiara and Show-Stopping Amethysts

It was a night of tiara triumphs as King Charles III and Queen Camilla hosted a state banquet in honor of the Sultan of Oman.

Kate Middleton and Prince William, Princess Anne and Sir Timothy Laurence, and the Duke and Duchess of Gloucester all donned their white tie finery for the Windsor Castle event.

The dress code called for tiaras, and the royal women did not disappoint. The Princess of Wales sparkled in her favourite Lover's Knot Tiara, Princess Anne returned once again to the Princess Andrew Meander, and the Duchess of Gloucester dazzled in the Gloucester Honeysuckle Tiara.

The Queen, meanwhile, paid tribute to Queen Elizabeth II in the Girls of Britain and Ireland Tiara, arguably the most famous piece in the late monarch's collection.

King Charles and Queen Camilla hosted the Sultan of Oman for a state banquet at Windsor Castle

But the adornments did not stop there. Queen Camilla added amethyst earrings that belonged to Queen Victoria's mother, the Duchess of Kent. Around her neck was a show-stopping amethyst necklace that has not previously been seen, although it is possible it was crafted using gems from the royal collection.

On Camilla's wrists were two more impressive heirloom jewels, namely two art deco Cartier bracelets that previously belonged to the Queen Mother. The bracelets are from a set of five different bracelets; two are entirely encrusted in diamonds and the other three feature diamonds alongside a different precious stone: sapphires, emeralds and rubies.

The bracelets were acquired in the 1920s by the Queen Mother when she was Duchess of York. She enjoyed wearing all five bracelets together, notes royal jewelry authority The Court Jeweller, particularly in the early years following her wedding to the Duke of York (later King George VI) in 1923. The bracelets could also be fashioned as a tiara: worn as three strands stacked one on top of the other.

Queen Camilla wore two of the Queen Mother's Art Deco Cartier bracelets to join King Charles III at the White House state banquet in March

The Art Deco bracelets later passed from the Queen Mother to her daughter, Queen Elizabeth II, who wore the bracelets as individual pieces on a number of occasions. One of her final outings in the jewels was to the Diplomatic Reception at Buckingham Palace in 2019.

In the years since the queen's death, they have become a regular fixture of Queen Camilla's wardrobe. The Queen often chooses the gems that best match the color of her outfit for a cohesive look.

The emerald bracelet, for example, proved the perfect choice when she was draped in a rich green dress for a reception celebrating Shakespeare at Buckingham Palace in 2024. Wearing red for the Qatar state banquet later that year, the ruby bracelet was the finishing touch. And for a Highgrove House event in 2025, the Queen accessorized her sapphire blue dress with actual sapphires.

Queen Elizabeth II wore the bracelets on several occasions, including the diamond and emerald piece for the Diplomatic Reception at Buckingham Palace in December 2019

This thematic dressing followed through to the state banquet in Washington, DC, in March 2026, where the diamond and ruby piece was the final glittering addition to her pink ensemble.

The Sultan of Oman was met in London by Prince William on Tuesday morning before the pair travelled together to Windsor, where the majority of the royal engagements took place. The Sultanate of Oman owns a number of London residences, including Nuffield Lodge, 35 Upper Brook Street, and the embassy at 167 Queen’s Gate.

The Sultan made his way to the Royal Dais on Dachet Road in Windsor, where he was officially welcomed by King Charles and Queen Camilla as the sound of a Royal Salute was fired from the Home Park and the Tower of London. The trio then joined the Prince and Princess of Wales for a carriage procession to Windsor Castle, as has become tradition in recent state visits.

At a reception celebrating Shakespeare in 2024, the Queen wore the emerald and diamond bracelet (just seen) as the finishing touch for her emerald dress

State visits are traditionally hosted at Buckingham Palace, but it remains under renovation.

Upon arrival in the Quadrangle of the Castle, the royals were greeted with a Royal Salute by a Guard of Honor while the regimental band played the Omani and British National Anthems. The Sultan and King Charles inspected the Guard before all five royals viewed a military parade. This was be followed by a lunch in the State Dining Room, with other members of the royal family, and a special exhibition of Oman-related items in the Green Drawing Room.

The high point of the state visit, however, was the state banquet. King Charles III and Queen Camilla were supported by the Prince and Princess of Wales, Princess Anne and Sir Timothy Laurence and the Duke and Duchess of Gloucester, as they welcomed the Sultan and some 150 esteemed guests to St George's Hall, Windsor Castle.

In late 2024, for the state banquet held in honour of the Emir of Qatar, Queen Camilla chose the ruby and diamond piece to offset her scarlet gown

#3797. Official WoW: Forever AMV Will Get You Super Hyped

World of Warcraft: Forever is almost here, and in case players weren't already excited to return to classic Azeroth, a new animated music video highlighting the war between the Horde and the Alliance is sure to get your inner player vs. player hyped up to spill some blood for your faction. 

Called "Warpath," the animated trailer made in collaboration between developer Blizzard and Brunch Studio features a banger of a track performed by YouTuber turned musician Poppy, Amy Lee of Evanescence fame, and Courtney LaPlante, the lead singer of Canadian metal band Spiritbox. The name of the song itself is "End of You," and its nu-metal vibes go a long way to invoke the early 2000s era that WoW originally spawned from.

The trailer shows members of the Horde (an orc Rogue, tauren Hunter, undead Paladin, troll Mage) and the Alliance (a human Warrior, gnome Warlock, dwarf Shaman, night elf Priest, and Skyborne Druid) duking it out on the slopes of Mount Hyjal, one of Forever's new zones.

Each class is wearing their iconic endgame dungeon armor set and can be seen wielding more than a few recognizable weapons, like Unstoppable Force, Dal'Rend's Sacred Charge and Tribal Guardian, and others. 

It's not the first time Blizzard has collaborated with Brunch on an AMV. Blizzard released "A Place To Call Home" performed by Aurora earlier this year ahead of the release of modern WoW's Midnight expansion, and it was quite the walk down memory lane for longtime players. 

Forever's launch may be right around the corner, but Blizzard isn't slowing down when it comes to announcements and changes. Most recently, it announced one last round of racial ability changes prior to launch day and the last-minute addition of another PvP server option. It will also make major improvements to the game's controversial layering system.

In an interview with GameSpot, Blizzard detailed how it's approaching Forever's endgame and what the future will hold for what will be the third major version of its enduring MMORPG. WoW: Forever launches November 4, with character creation and name reservations going live October 27 for those who purchased the game's new optional playable race, Skyborne elves.

#3795. Savannah Chrisley Defends 'Huge' Age Gap With Fiance: 'I Don't Care'

Savannah Chrisley has no time for anyone’s opinion on her relationship with an older man.

“Yes, there is a huge age gap there, OK?” Chrisley, 29, said on the Tuesday, October 27, episode of her “Unlocked” podcast, announcing her engagement to fiancé Jason and revealing her pregnancy. “And that’s probably part of the reason why I kept a lot of it quiet, because I know how brutal the public can be. But I genuinely do not care.”

Chrisley continued, “I have always said, ‘I would rather [have] 20 phenomenal, unbelievable years with someone than 50 just [fine] years.’ Like, the amount of love and respect that we have, we have so many dreams and we dream together, we work together … we want to have life experiences with each other. And I think that’s when you know you have the right person, when you don’t feel like you have to escape them at times.”

The former reality star referred to Jason as her “best friend,” adding, “We do everything together. … And it’s just such an amazing, fulfilling feeling to finally find your person. And I’m there. So yes, the age difference. So what?”

Chrisley also pointed out that her fiancé “does not look his age, nor does he act his age.”

“His body is great. His mind is even sharper. So I really don’t care, OK?” she added. “Like, this person is the love of my life. And it made all the heartbreak that I’ve gone through worth it. And all the heartbreak taught me something. It taught me what I want, what I don’t want, what I should accept, what I shouldn’t accept.”

Chrisley announced via Instagram on Tuesday that she is pregnant with the couple’s first baby. While she’s shared limited information about Jason’s identity, she gave “Unlocked” listeners more details about his “magical” proposal in the Cayman Islands.

“When he gave me the ring, he was like, ‘Before I open this, I just want you to know that this is just an expression of love to you. This can be whatever you want it to be. This can be an engagement ring, a promise ring, a cocktail ring for all I care. I just want you to know how much I love you,'” she recalled on the podcast. “And there was no pressure ever put on me to, like, ‘You have to accept this as this.'”

Chrisley gushed over her “insane” ring, saying the design is “everything I ever wanted.” She also acknowledged that the engagement was “really quick,” but she insisted, “Timing means nothing.”

Before announcing the pair’s milestones, Chrisley opened up about dating an older man, revealing in June that Jason is “a financial guy” who she met through “the political world of D.C.”

“You see, age-gap relationships are the new thing. … There’s all these couples that [have] a huge age-gap relationship, but it just works, like, it’s insane,” she said on the “Whine Down” podcast, joking that her dad, Todd Chrisley, wasn’t “fond” of the age difference at first. “I love it because you’re not dealing with the insecurity of a younger guy who’s trying to keep up.”

Savannah noted at the time that this was “the healthiest relationship” she’s ever been in. “In the year we’ve been together, we’ve never yelled at each other, we’ve never name-called, none of that, but [we] still go to therapy together because we’re like, ‘Let’s do it before we actually need to do it,'” she explained. “It’s the greatest.”

#3794. It’s Still October, But I Think I Found My Favorite Hallmark Christmas Movie Of 2026

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Keep exploring and earning more as a member.

Discover the latest entertainment updates and trailers.

Sydney Sweeney Just Fantasy Cast Herself As Cindy Lou Who For Unexpected Holiday Lingerie Ad

I'm Really Bummed Jenna Ortega's New Movie Falls In Line With A 'Rotten' Trend She's On With Critics

New And Recent Movies Streaming: Big Films We Can Finally Watch At Home - Updated October 2026

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

I don't think any movie will beat this one!

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

We’re still days away from Halloween, but that hasn’t stopped the Hallmark Channel from already starting to build momentum for the Christmas season. The cable channel and streaming service’s Countdown to Christmas lineup has officially hit the 2026 TV schedule and has already delivered nine heartwarming romances. While there are still over thirty movies set to release, including the highly anticipated Lacey Chabert-led flick set at Disney World, I think I already found my favorite new 2026 holiday movie: Winter Wonderlanes.

The film follows Audrey (Ashley Newbrough), a “globe-trotting pop-up restaurateur” whose quick trip home for Thanksgiving turns into an extended stay when she decides to help her grandmother get her late grandfather’s beloved bowling alley in tip-top condition to sell. Begrudgingly, she teams up with local news reporter (and her former high school boyfriend) Jason (Franco Lo Presti) to get more eyes on the bowling alley in the hopes it sells for an impressive price. Things get complicated when Audrey starts to remember just how important Wonderlanes is to her and how endearing Jason always was — this is a Hallmark movie after all.

I know, I know, the synopsis doesn’t exactly scream Christmas, but I promise Winter Wonderlanes follows Hallmark’s tried-and-true perfect Christmas movie formula. Here are three more reasons why I think it’s the best movie on this year’s lineup.

It’s no secret that Hallmark loves to use the same ideas and tropes over and over, and Winter Wonderlanes is no exception. From the very beginning of the film, it's clear that audiences are being thrust into a second-chance romance between Audrey and Jason.

Overall, it's a tough trope to pull off since there has to be a good explanation for why the relationship ended and a compelling reason why they should try again. It doesn’t always work, but considering Audrey and Jason were high school sweethearts, I found it easy to forgive their past mistakes since they were still children.

It’s clear that Audrey and Jason are still harboring feelings for each other, but I really enjoyed the slow-burn aspect of their reconnection. Through patience and the power of honest communication, these two were able to work through their past grievances, and they end up in a committed relationship.

One of the biggest complaints when it comes to Hallmark movies is that the settings are almost always the same: small towns or a royal location. While Winter Wonderlanes does take place in a small town — it would be sacrilegious if it didn’t — the majority of the story takes place in the Wonderlanes bowling alley.

Bowling alleys don’t scream Christmas, but Audrey manages to transform the place into a festive place with the help of her late grandfather’s decorations. This particular backdrop also makes those staple Hallmark Christmas movie scenes like decorating a tree and the first kiss feel fun and fresh since they happen within the walls of the business.

I haven’t been bowling in years, but this movie definitely has me adding it to my list of things to do this holiday season.

Audrey and Jason’s relationship is definitely the biggest selling point of the film, but Winter Wonderlanes also exceeds expectations when it comes to the secondary plots. In addition to the aforementioned characters' love story, the movie has a heartwarming young-adult love story blooming in the background between Audrey’s cousin and Jason’s young cameraman.

Should The Holidays Come Twice A Year? I Asked One Hallmark Star How She Feels About Christmas In July

The film also explores the complex feelings around grief. Said themes are conveyed through Audrey’s feelings about returning to the bowling alley without her grandfather present, and in the way both Audrey and Jason have to deal with the loss of what their relationship could have been had they been honest with each other as teens.

Surprisingly, I also enjoyed the more business-driven aspects of the plot. Not only is Audrey trying to get the bowling alley ready to sell alongside her high school best friend, but she’s also busy helping her endearing business partner-turned-best friend land the perfect location for their next restaurant venture. And of course, those two besties might even find love together too. (I definitely smell a sequel!)

I know I’m getting ahead of myself since there are still plenty of movies to come on the Countdown to Christmas schedule that I’m excited for. Yet, for now, I’m confident in declaring Winter Wonderlanes the best film of the lineup.

If you happen to miss the movie, you can stream it on the Hallmark Plus app with an active subscription. Or, catch it again on the cable channel on November 1st at 2 p.m. ET. And don’t forget to check out the 2026 Christmas movie schedule that covers all the streaming services.

Danielle Bruncati is a writer and pop culture enthusiast from Southern California. She earned a Master of Fine Arts degree in Television Writing and Producing from a top film school. Her goal is to one day be the writer on a show/movie covered by Cinemablend, but for now, she's excited to be a Freelance Writer here.

Danielle watches just about everything, but her favorite shows and movies often land in the YA and romantic comedy spaces. When she's not writing, she can be found wandering around Disneyland or hanging out with her laughter-hating corgi.
